Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. Um, well, I guess I'm just having a little trouble staying focused lately? It's probably nothing major, just normal everyday stuff, you know? 

Like, I'll be trying to fill out a job application, and then, I don't know, a car will drive by outside, or my phone will buzz, or I'll just get a random thought in my head, and suddenly I'm doing something completely different. I don't even realize I've stopped working on the application until later. It's just... I get pulled off track by every little thing. Is that something people usually come in for? I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of nothing.

Um, I guess it’s been... well, really noticeable for about a year and ten months? Give or take?

Um... maybe a little bit? I mean, I think it's pretty normal to need to move around sometimes, right?

Um, well... I guess I was a little bit energetic when I was younger? But I think most kids are, right? I really tried to be a good student, I promise.

Um, well... I guess organization isn't my absolute strongest skill? I mean, I really try to keep things neat and get my chores done, I promise. I want to be helpful and productive.

But, um, sometimes if I have a bunch of things on my plate... like, say I need to call the counselor, and clean up my room, and maybe work on sending in one of those applications... I just kind of look at it all and I'm not really sure which one is the most important to start with. So I'll sort of start cleaning, and then remember the application, so I go look at that, and then I think about the phone call... and I just kind of bounce around between them. And then at the end of the day, I've got a bunch of things half-finished. 

I'm really trying to get better at making lists and sticking to them, though! I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring my responsibilities. It just gets a little jumbled in my head sometimes. Is it okay to struggle with figuring out where to start when you have a lot to do?

I guess my hands just like to be busy? \n\nI hope I'm not doing it too much right now, I know it can be a bad habit. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking or blurt out answers before they've finished asking a question?\n\nPatient: Um, I try really hard not to be rude, but... yeah, I guess I do that sometimes? It's definitely not on purpose, I promise. \n\nIt's just, like, if I'm talking to my mom, or, um, the counselor I was seeing, they'll be asking me something and the answer will just pop right into my head. And before I can even stop myself, it just kind of tumbles out of my mouth before they're even finished talking. I always feel really bad about it afterwards because I know it's not polite to cut people off. I really am working on trying to wait my turn to speak. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as disrespectful or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like doing something sudden without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I try to be pretty careful about things, you know? \n\nI think everyone has moments where they might do something a little spontaneous, which is okay, right? But I wouldn't say I just do things without thinking about what might happen. I really try to be responsible and make good choices. I don't want to cause any trouble or make things harder for anyone else. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just... I really do try to think things through as best as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems at work, school, or in your relationships due to your difficulty staying focused or other behaviors we've discussed?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try my best to stay on top of things, I promise. \n\nBut, um, I have been trying to become an automotive technician. I'm really good at the hands-on stuff, you know? I know I can do the actual work. It's just... I guess I've missed a few training deadlines here and there. And, um, I've had to redo some applications because I missed some details the first time around. It's just a little overwhelming sometimes, but I don't want you to think I'm lazy or not trying. I really do want to get a good job and be responsible. I just kind of look at it all and I'm not really sure which one is the most important to start with. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I mean... I guess I can pay attention just fine when I need to, you know? Like, if someone is talking to me or if I'm supposed to be doing something, I try really hard to be present and respectful. 

I don't think I ever get so wrapped up in something that I just ignore people or forget what's going on around me. I'd hate to make anyone feel like I'm ignoring them or that I don't care about what they're saying. I always try to be responsive when my family needs me. 

I mean, I might get into a video game or a project for a bit, but I can always snap out of it if someone calls my name. That's the right way to be, right? You're supposed to be able to pull yourself away when people need you?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... yeah, I guess they were. I mean, I really don't want to sound like I was a bad kid or anything, because I always meant well! I really wanted to be a good student. 

But, um... even back in elementary school, my teachers were, well, they were always having to move my seat. I guess I had a bad habit of talking out of turn, and... and I'd forget my homework a lot. I'd also kind of rush through my worksheets and make a bunch of careless mistakes. I wasn't trying to be disrespectful or lazy, I promise! I just... I don't know, I always felt bad when the teachers got frustrated with me. 

I've always tried really hard to do better, though. It's okay that I struggled a little bit when I was younger, right? Like, a lot of kids have trouble sitting still and doing homework, don't they?

I know a lot of people deal with injuries and bounce right back, so I feel a little silly that it messed up my focus so much, but yeah... almost two years. Is that a helpful timeline for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um... maybe a little bit? I mean, I think it's pretty normal to need to move around sometimes, right? \n\nBut I guess, yeah, if I’m sitting with someone, my leg is usually bouncing. Or, um, I’ll be clicking a pen, or just kind of messing with my keys in my pocket. The thing is, I don't even realize I’m doing it most of the time until I catch myself. I really try hard not to be annoying or distracting to other people with it, it's just... I guess my hands just like to be busy? \n\nI hope I'm not doing it too much right now, I know it can be a bad habit. Is that the kind of thing you're asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking or blurt out answers before they've finished asking a question?\n\nPatient: Um, I try really hard not to be rude, but... yeah, I guess I do that sometimes? It's definitely not on purpose, I promise. \n\nIt's just, like, if I'm talking to my mom, or, um, the counselor I was seeing, they'll be asking me something and the answer will just pop right into my head. And before I can even stop myself, it just kind of tumbles out of my mouth before they're even finished talking. I always feel really bad about it afterwards because I know it's not polite to cut people off. I really am working on trying to wait my turn to speak. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not coming across as disrespectful or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like doing something sudden without considering the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I try to be pretty careful about things, you know? \n\nI think everyone has moments where they might do something a little spontaneous, which is okay, right? But I wouldn't say I just do things without thinking about what might happen. I really try to be responsible and make good choices. I don't want to cause any trouble or make things harder for anyone else. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just... I really do try to think things through as best as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems at work, school, or in your relationships due to your difficulty staying focused or other behaviors we've discussed?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I really try my best to stay on top of things, I promise. \n\nBut, um, I have been trying to become an automotive technician. I'm really good at the hands-on stuff, you know? I know I can do the actual work. It's just... I guess I've missed a few training deadlines here and there. And, um, I've had to redo some applications because I missed some details the first time around. So I haven't really moved forward with it as much as I'd like to yet. \n\nI'm really trying to get better at keeping track of all that paperwork stuff, though. It's just a little overwhelming sometimes, but I don't want you to think I'm lazy or not trying. I really do want to get a good job and be responsible. Is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Were you often restless or fidgety when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I was a little bit energetic when I was younger? But I think most kids are, right? I really tried to be a good student, I promise. \n\nBut, um, I guess even back in elementary school, my teachers were always having to move my seat around. It wasn't because I was trying to be bad or anything! It's just... I guess I talked out of turn sometimes, and I'd forget my homework, or I'd rush through my worksheets and make some careless mistakes. I always felt terrible when they got frustrated with me. I really wanted them to like me and think I was smart. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a troublemaker. I really did want to do well in school, it was just hard to sit still and do everything exactly right all the time. Is that okay? Like, is it normal for kids to struggle with that kind of stuff?\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities as a child?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... maybe a little? I mean, I really enjoy hanging out with my family and stuff, you know? I want to be part of what everyone's doing. \n\nBut, um, I guess quiet stuff is kind of hard for me. Like, if my family is all watching a movie together, or if I'm just trying to sit and read a book... I don't know, I just sort of end up talking, or I need to get up to grab something, or I might be tapping my foot or making some noise. I really don't mean to bother anyone, I swear! I just... I get a little antsy, I guess. I try really hard to be quiet and let them enjoy the movie, but it just kind of happens before I realize it. \n\nI don't want to ruin things for everyone else. Do you think that's super annoying? I really try to be respectful of people's quiet time, I just struggle with it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty organizing tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess organization isn't my absolute strongest skill? I mean, I really try to keep things neat and get my chores done, I promise. I want to be helpful and productive.\n\nBut, um, sometimes if I have a bunch of things on my plate... like, say I need to call the counselor, and clean up my room, and maybe work on sending in one of those applications... I just kind of look at it all and I'm not really sure which one is the most important to start with. So I'll sort of start cleaning, and then remember the application, so I go look at that, and then I think about the phone call... and I just kind of bounce around between them. And then at the end of the day, I've got a bunch of things half-finished. \n\nI'm really trying to get better at making lists and sticking to them, though! I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring my responsibilities. It just gets a little jumbled in my head sometimes. Is it okay to struggle with figuring out where to start when you have a lot to do?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant feelings of anxiety, sadness, or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stressed or bummed out sometimes, right? But I definitely wouldn't say it's anything significant or... or bad. \n\nI really try to stay positive and keep a good attitude about things. Even when I get a little overwhelmed with, you know, my chores or trying to figure out what to do next, I don't let it get me down too much. I don't want to be a burden on anyone or bring the mood down. I'm usually pretty okay, I promise. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? Like, it's normal to just feel okay most of the time, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so intensely focused on something that you lose track of everything else around you?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean... I guess I can pay attention just fine when I need to, you know? Like, if someone is talking to me or if I'm supposed to be doing something, I try really hard to be present and respectful. \n\nI don't think I ever get so wrapped up in something that I just ignore people or forget what's going on around me. I'd hate to make anyone feel like I'm ignoring them or that I don't care about what they're saying. I always try to be responsive when my family needs me. \n\nI mean, I might get into a video game or a project for a bit, but I can always snap out of it if someone calls my name. That's the right way to be, right? You're supposed to be able to pull yourself away when people need you?\n\nDoctor: Were your focus and behavior issues noticeable to you or others before your soccer injury?\n\nPatient: Um, well... yeah, I guess they were. Um, well... I guess socially I do okay. I mean, I really try to be a good friend and be there for people. I don't want to be a burden on anyone, you know?

Um, well, yeah... I think we talked about this a little bit earlier, but... yes. Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, my parents never really said anything about me being, like, a difficult toddler or anything. 

I think I was just a regular little kid, you know? Maybe a little busy sometimes, but my mom always said I was just a happy, curious boy. They never took me to a doctor or got mad at me for how I acted before I started school. I really tried to be good for them, even when I was tiny.

Is it normal to struggle with that kind of stuff sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Were you often restless or fidgety when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I was a little bit energetic when I was younger? But I think most kids are, right? I really tried to be a good student, I promise. \n\nBut, um, I guess even back in elementary school, my teachers were always having to move my seat around. It wasn't because I was trying to be bad or anything! It's just... I guess I talked out of turn sometimes, and I'd forget my homework, or I'd rush through my worksheets and make some careless mistakes. I always felt terrible when they got frustrated with me. I really wanted them to like me and think I was smart. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a troublemaker. I really did want to do well in school, it was just hard to sit still and do everything exactly right all the time. Is that okay? Like, is it normal for kids to struggle with that kind of stuff?\n\nDoctor: Did you have difficulty playing quietly or engaging in leisure activities as a child?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... maybe a little? Like, if my family is all watching a movie together, or if I'm just trying to sit and read a book... Do you think that's super annoying? I really try to be respectful of people's quiet time, I just struggle with it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty organizing tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I want to be helpful and productive.\n\nBut, um, sometimes if I have a bunch of things on my plate... like, say I need to call the counselor, and clean up my room, and maybe work on sending in one of those applications... I just kind of look at it all and I'm not really sure which one is the most important to start with. So I'll sort of start cleaning, and then remember the application, so I go look at that, and then I think about the phone call... and I just kind of bounce around between them. And then at the end of the day, I've got a bunch of things half-finished. \n\nI'm really trying to get better at making lists and sticking to them, though! I don't want you to think I'm just ignoring my responsibilities. It just gets a little jumbled in my head sometimes. Oh, um, before I was twelve? So, like, during elementary school? 

Well... I guess maybe around then, things were a little bit different. I really hope you don't think I was a troublemaker or anything, because I promise I wasn't trying to be!
